(lines 1135-1225)\n  │   ├── ssh_transport:handle_packet_part/5 → decryption\n  │   └── ssh_message:decode/1 → message parsing\n  │\n  └── Message dispatch (lines 1156-1182)\n      ├── #ssh_msg_kexinit{} → kexinit handler\n      ├── #ssh_msg_*auth*{} → auth FSM\n      └── #ssh_msg_channel_*{} → {conn_msg, Msg} event\n```\n\n### 9. **Message Flow Example: Password Authentication**\n\n1. **Client sends** `#ssh_msg_userauth_request{method = \"password\", ...}`\n2. **`ssh_message:decode/1`** parses binary to record\n3. **`ssh_connection_handler` routes** to `{userauth, server}` state\n4. **`ssh_fsm_userauth_server:handle_event/4`** matches message\n5. **`ssh_auth:handle_userauth_request/3`** extracts password\n6. **`check_password/3`** invokes callback to verify credentials\n7. **Returns** `{authorized, User, ...}` or `{not_authorized, ...}`\n8. **`ssh_fsm_userauth_server`** transitions to `{connected, server}`\n9. **`ssh_message:encode/1`** encodes `#ssh_msg_userauth_success{}`\n10. **Sent back** to client via encrypted channel\n\nThis comprehensive architecture ensures secure, protocol-compliant SSH communication with multiple authentication methods, proper state management, and extensible channel handling."}],"totalDurationMs":79173,"totalTokens":56317,"totalToolUseCount":41,"usage":{"input_tokens":3,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1680,"cache_read_input_tokens":54633,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1680,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1}}}

This vulnerability allows unauthenticated attackers to gain full system access by sending crafted SSH packets before any login or credentials are provided.\n\n### Technical Details\n\nThe vulnerability lies in how Erlang/OTP's built-in SSH server processes connection protocol messages during the pre-authentication phase.(no content)In CVE-2025-32433, the Erlang/OTP SSH daemon fails to enforce this rule. The attacker sends this request before authentication, and the server incorrectly processes it, allowing a session channel to be opened without verifying the user's identity.(no content)Due to improper pre-auth validation, the server accepts and processes the SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with an \"exec\" payload even though no authentication has occurred. This enables remote, unauthenticated attackers to execute arbitrary shell commands on the server.\n\n### Affected Versions\n\nVulnerable versions include Erlang/OTP prior to OTP-27.3.3, OTP-26.2.5.11 and OTP-25.3.2.20.\n\n### Impact\n\nThe consequences of CVE-2025-32433 are potentially severe because it grants code execution before authentication allowing a threat actor to effectively become an unauthorized system admin on the target device. Full System Compromise: Any command the threat actor sends will run with the privileges of the SSH service process. In many Erlang/OTP deployments, the SSH daemon runs as root, so the threat actor gains absolute control.(no content)Because no valid credentials or user interaction are needed, the attack can happen with no warning signs, like failed logins. It's a pre-auth compromise that may not trigger usual authentication logs.\n\n### Affected Systems and Industries\n\nErlang/OTP is a foundational technology in high-availability systems used across telecommunications, messaging platforms, IoT infrastructure, and financial services. It is used by organizations like Ericsson, Cisco, and WhatsApp.(no content)Attackers are attempting to exploit the vulnerability in short, high-intensity bursts. These are disproportionately targeting OT networks and attempting to access exposed services over both IT and industrial ports. Early telemetry confirms that the threat extends far beyond traditional industrial sectors, impacting education, healthcare and high technology.\n\n### Patched Versions\n\nA patch is available in Erlang/OTP versions OTP-27.3.3, OTP-26.2.5.11, OTP-25.3.2.20 and later.\n\n### Mitigations\n\nFor users unable to immediately upgrade to a fixed version, Erlang recommends disabling the SSH server or restricting access using firewall rules.(no content)While fixes for Erlang/OTP SSH are now available, the security patch is not automatically applied to software products that use Erlang/OTP SSH. The best method for remediating these vulnerabilities in third-party software products is to apply the official security updates from the vendor of each affected software product.(no content)On April 16, 2025, a critical vulnerability in the Erlang/OTP SSH server was disclosed. This vulnerability could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to perform remote code execution (RCE) on an affected device. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_vrtx_01NSMirhcZT4PjLsxctLy7dP","type":"tool_result","content":"# CVE-2025-32433 Technical Analysis\n\n## Vulnerability Core Issue\n\nThe vulnerability stems from improper state enforcement in Erlang/OTP's SSH daemon. The fundamental flaw is that \"SSH connection protocol messages (codes >= 80)\" are processed before authentication completes, when \"should only be processed after successful authentication.\"\n\n## SSH Message Protocol Involved\n\nThe exploit leverages SSH connection protocol messages—those with message codes 80 and above in the SSH protocol specification. These messages are part of RFC 4254 (SSH Connection Protocol) and should only execute after successful user authentication per RFC 4252 (SSH Authentication Protocol).\n\n## Exploitation Mechanism\n\nAn unauthenticated attacker can send these post-authentication messages directly to open SSH ports without credentials, triggering command execution. This creates a critical authentication bypass where the daemon fails to validate that authentication has completed before processing privileged operations.\n\n## Patching Strategy\n\nThe fix involves enforcing proper state validation. Affected versions must upgrade to:\n- OTP 27.3.3 or later\n- OTP 26.2.5.11 or later  \n- OTP 25.3.2.20 or later\n\nThese patches restore correct state checking to reject SSH connection protocol messages until authentication succeeds, restoring the intended security boundary between unauthenticated and authenticated protocol phases."}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"77b13f6a-f78b-4e6c-a94a-1e8d9e2cd081","uuid":"9244d37c-4200-45d3-a557-b90a13e6140d","tool_use_result":{"bytes":256887,"code":200,"codeText":"OK","result":"# CVE-2025-32433 Technical Analysis\n\n## Vulnerability Core Issue\n\nThe vulnerability stems from improper state enforcement in Erlang/OTP's SSH daemon. This creates a critical authentication bypass where the daemon fails to validate that authentication has completed before processing privileged operations.\n\n## Patching Strategy\n\nThe fix involves enforcing proper state validation. It follows the SSH protocol specifications defined in RFC 4250-4254.\n\n## SSH Protocol Layers\n\nThe SSH protocol consists of three main layers:\n\n### 1. Transport Layer (RFC 4253)\n- Handles initial key exchange\n- Provides encryption, integrity, and authentication\n- Message codes: 1-49\n\n### 2. Authentication Layer (RFC 4252)\n- Handles user authentication\n- Password, public key, keyboard-interactive methods\n- Message codes: 50-79\n\n### 3. Connection Layer (RFC 4254)\n- Handles channels and sessions\n- Executes commands, opens terminals\n- **Message codes: 80 and higher**\n\n## Key Source Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `ssh_connection_handler.erl` | Central state machine managing connection lifecycle |\n| `ssh_connection.erl` | SSH Connection Protocol (RFC 4254) implementation |\n| `ssh_auth.erl` | Authentication method implementations |\n| `ssh_transport.erl` | Transport layer encryption/decryption |\n| `ssh_message.erl` | Binary encoding/decoding of SSH messages |\n| `ssh_connect.hrl` | Connection protocol message records |\n\n## SSH Message Codes (RFC 4254 - Connection Protocol)\n\n```\nSSH_MSG_GLOBAL_REQUEST            80\nSSH_MSG_REQUEST_SUCCESS           81\nSSH_MSG_REQUEST_FAILURE           82\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN              90\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION 91\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_FAILURE      92\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_WINDOW_ADJUST     93\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_DATA              94\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EXTENDED_DATA     95\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_EOF               96\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_CLOSE             97\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST           98\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS           99\nSSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE           100\n```\n\n## Critical Security Rule (RFC 4252 Section 6)\n\n> Message numbers of 80 and higher are reserved for protocols running after this authentication protocol, so receiving one of them before authentication is complete is an error, to which the server MUST respond by disconnecting.\n\nThis means:\n- Before authentication completes, only messages 1-79 are valid\n- Messages 80+ (connection layer) should ONLY be processed AFTER successful authentication\n- Receiving message 80+ before auth = immediate disconnect\n\n## Connection Handler State Machine\n\nThe SSH connection follows these states:\n```\nwait_for_socket\n    ↓\n{hello, Role}\n    ↓\n{service_request, Role}\n    ↓\n{userauth, Role}  ← Authentication phase (messages 50-79)\n    ↓\n{connected, Role}  ← Authenticated! Now messages 80+ are valid\n```\n\n## SSH Record Structure\n\nThe `#ssh{}` record contains connection state:\n\n```erlang\n#ssh{\n    authenticated = true | false,  % Is user authenticated?\n    role = server | client,\n    opts = [...],                  % Options\n    session_id = binary(),\n    ...\n}\n```\n\n## Message Handling Pattern\n\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(Message, Connection, Role, SSH_Params) ->\n    %% Pattern match on different message types\n    %% Route to appropriate handlers\n```\n\n## Example: Channel Open Message\n\n```erlang\n#ssh_msg_channel_open{\n    channel_type = \"session\",\n    sender_channel = ChannelId,\n    initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n    maximum_packet_size = PacketSize\n}\n```\n\n## Example: Channel Request Message (exec)\n\n```erlang\n#ssh_msg_channel_request{\n    recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n    request_type = \"exec\",\n    want_reply = true,\n    data = CommandBinary\n}\n```\n\n## Disconnection\n\nTo disconnect a client:\n```erlang\n{disconnect, {?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, \"Reason\"}, handle_stop(Connection)}\n```\n\nCommon disconnect codes:\n- `?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` - Protocol violation\n- `?SSH_DISCONNECT_BY_APPLICATION` - Normal shutdown\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":248,"cache_read_input_tokens":28874,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":248,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"77b13f6a-f78b-4e6c-a94a-1e8d9e2cd081","uuid":"ccbf23dd-01bf-449f-9127-f8f9e4a88f22"}

Attacker connects to SSH server\n2. Completes key exchange (transport layer)\n3. **Skips authentication entirely**\n4. Sends `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN` (message code 90) to open a channel\n5. Sends `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST` (message code 98) with type \"exec\"\n6. The server executes the command via `os:cmd/1` function\n7. Attacker gains RCE with privileges of the SSH daemon process\n\n## Impact\n\n- Full system compromise without credentials\n- Arbitrary command execution\n- Often runs as root, granting complete control\n- No authentication logs generated (attack happens pre-auth)\n\n## Exploitation Example\n\nThe attack sends these SSH messages in sequence:\n1. SSH_MSG_KEXINIT - Standard key exchange\n2. SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN - Opens channel (should be rejected pre-auth)\n3. SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST type=\"exec\" - Executes command\n\n## The Fix\n\nThe fix adds a check in `ssh_connection.erl` to verify authentication before processing any connection-layer message:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(Msg, Connection, server, Ssh = #ssh{authenticated = false}) ->\n    %% See RFC4252 6.\n    %% Message numbers of 80 and higher are reserved for protocols running\n    %% after this authentication protocol, so receiving one of them before\n    %% authentication is complete is an error, to which the server MUST\n    %% respond by disconnecting.\n    {disconnect, {?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, \"Connection refused\"},\n     handle_stop(Connection)};\n```\n\nThis pattern-matching clause:\n1. Matches any message (`Msg`) arriving on the server side\n2. Checks if `authenticated = false` in the SSH state\n3. Returns a disconnect response with a protocol error\n\nThe clause must be placed at the **end** of the `handle_msg/4` function to act as a catch-all for unauthenticated connection messages.\n\n## Key Files Modified\n\n- `lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` - Main fix location\n\n## References\n\n- [NVD CVE-2025-32433](https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-32433)\n- [Erlang OTP Patch Commit](https://github.com/erlang/otp/commit/6eef04130afc8b0ccb63c9a0d8650209cf54892f)\n- RFC 4252 Section 6 - Authentication Protocol Specification\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1190,"cache_read_input_tokens":29122,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1190,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"77b13f6a-f78b-4e6c-a94a-1e8d9e2cd081","uuid":"ba3f2bd4-de51-4316-8893-dda045457170"}

Understanding this is essential for modifying Erlang code correctly.\n\n## Function Clause Order\n\nIn Erlang, function clauses are tried **in order from top to bottom**. The first matching clause is executed.\n\n```erlang\n%% Specific cases first\nhandle(specific_case) -> do_specific();\nhandle(another_case) -> do_another();\n%% General catch-all last\nhandle(_) -> do_default().\n```\n\n**Important**: More specific patterns should come BEFORE more general patterns.\n\n## Pattern Matching with Records\n\nRecords are named tuples. Pattern matching extracts fields:\n\n```erlang\n%% Define record\n-record(ssh, {authenticated = false, role, opts}).\n\n%% Match specific field values\nhandle(Request, #ssh{authenticated = true}) ->\n    %% Only matches when authenticated = true\n    process(Request);\n\nhandle(Request, #ssh{authenticated = false}) ->\n    %% Only matches when authenticated = false\n    reject(Request).\n```\n\n## Guard Expressions\n\nGuards add additional conditions after pattern matching:\n\n```erlang\nhandle(Msg, Ssh) when Ssh#ssh.authenticated == true ->\n    process(Msg);\n\nhandle(Msg, Ssh) when Ssh#ssh.authenticated == false ->\n    reject(Msg).\n```\n\n## Multiple Function Arguments\n\nPattern match across multiple arguments:\n\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(Msg, Connection, Role, Ssh) ->\n    ...\n\n%% Match specific combination\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{}, Conn, server, #ssh{authenticated = true}) ->\n    %% Channel open from authenticated server connection\n    open_channel(Conn);\n\nhandle_msg(_, Conn, server, #ssh{authenticated = false}) ->\n    %% Any message from unauthenticated server = reject\n    disconnect(Conn).\n```\n\n## Adding New Clauses\n\nWhen adding a new function clause, consider:\n\n1. **Specificity**: More specific patterns first\n2. **Catch-all placement**: General patterns at the end\n3. **Variable naming**: Use `_Var` for unused variables to avoid warnings\n\n## Example: Security Check Addition\n\nBefore (vulnerable):\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{...}, Conn, Role, Ssh) ->\n    open_channel(...);\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{...}, Conn, Role, Ssh) ->\n    process_request(...).\n```\n\nAfter (secure):\n```erlang\n%% Specific handlers for authenticated users\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{...}, Conn, Role,\n           Ssh = #ssh{authenticated = true}) ->\n    open_channel(...);\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{...}, Conn, Role,\n           Ssh = #ssh{authenticated = true}) ->\n    process_request(...);\n\n%% Catch-all for unauthenticated - MUST BE LAST\nhandle_msg(_Msg, Conn, server, #ssh{authenticated = false}) ->\n    {disconnect, {?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, \"Connection refused\"},\n     handle_stop(Conn)}.\n```\n\n## Clause Termination\n\n- Use `;` (semicolon) to separate clauses of the same function\n- Use `.` (period) to end the final clause\n\n```erlang\nfunc(a) -> result_a;   %% semicolon - more clauses follow\nfunc(b) -> result_b;   %% semicolon - more clauses follow\nfunc(_) -> default.    %% period - last clause\n```\n\n## Common Macros in SSH\n\n```erlang\n?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR   %% Disconnect reason code\n?GET_OPT(Key, Opts)              %% Get option from options list\n?LOG_DEBUG(Format, Args)         %% Debug logging macro\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1030,"cache_read_input_tokens":30312,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1030,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"77b13f6a-f78b-4e6c-a94a-1e8d9e2cd081","uuid":"734e769a-f2fe-4b4b-bcb6-94bc75546f60"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_011wB3h1SQCEtcns8cS4Rbqk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01GLHZK1cWsVF4pEwht1MsJq","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/workspace/environment/skills/ssh-security-best-practices.md","content":"# SSH Server Security Best Practices\n\n## Authentication Enforcement\n\n### The Cardinal Rule\n\n**Never process privileged operations before authentication completes.**\n\nThis applies to:\n- Channel operations (open, request, data)\n- Command execution\n- Port forwarding\n- Subsystem access\n\n### State Machine Design\n\nA secure SSH server should enforce states:\n\n```\n1. TRANSPORT → Only key exchange messages\n2. USERAUTH  → Only authentication messages (50-79)\n3. CONNECTED → All messages including connection layer (80+)\n```\n\nState transitions must be explicit and validated.\n\n### Message Validation Pattern\n\n```erlang\nhandle_message(Msg, State) ->\n    case {message_type(Msg), State#state.authenticated} of\n        {connection_msg, false} ->\n            %% REJECT - connection messages before auth\n            disconnect(protocol_error);\n        {connection_msg, true} ->\n            %% OK - user is authenticated\n            process(Msg);\n        {auth_msg, _} ->\n            %% Auth messages always allowed during handshake\n            handle_auth(Msg)\n    end.\n```\n\n## Common SSH Vulnerabilities\n\n### 1. Pre-Authentication Command Execution (CVE-2025-32433)\n- **Issue**: Connection messages processed before auth\n- **Fix**: Check `authenticated` flag before processing\n\n### 2. Channel ID Confusion\n- **Issue**: Using client-provided channel IDs without validation\n- **Fix**: Validate channel ownership\n\n### 3. Buffer Overflow in Packet Handling\n- **Issue**: Not checking packet size limits\n- **Fix**: Enforce `maximum_packet_size`\n\n### 4. Weak Algorithm Negotiation\n- **Issue**: Accepting deprecated algorithms\n- **Fix**: Configure allowed algorithms list\n\n## Defense in Depth\n\n1. **Check authentication state** at multiple layers\n2. **Validate message sequence** (no out-of-order messages)\n3. **Log suspicious activity** (unexpected messages)\n4. **Rate limit** connection attempts\n5. **Timeout** idle connections\n\n## Secure Disconnect\n\nWhen detecting a protocol violation:\n\n```erlang\n{disconnect,\n {?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, \"Descriptive reason\"},\n cleanup_state(Connection)}\n```\n\n- Use appropriate disconnect code\n- Log the violation\n- Clean up connection state\n- Don't leak sensitive information in error messages\n\n## Testing Security Fixes\n\n1. **Positive test**: Normal authentication still works\n2. **Negative test**: Pre-auth messages are rejected\n3. **Boundary test**: Check exact state transition points\n4. **Fuzzing**: Random message sequences\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":13,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1081,"cache_read_input_tokens":31342,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1081,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"77b13f6a-f78b-4e6c-a94a-1e8d9e2cd081","uuid":"5889f9b8-1336-434b-b0e8-9914624f40e8"}

TRANSPORT → Only key exchange messages\n2. USERAUTH  → Only authentication messages (50-79)\n3. CONNECTED → All messages including connection layer (80+)\n```\n\nState transitions must be explicit and validated.\n\n### Message Validation Pattern\n\n```erlang\nhandle_message(Msg, State) ->\n    case {message_type(Msg), State#state.authenticated} of\n        {connection_msg, false} ->\n            %% REJECT - connection messages before auth\n            disconnect(protocol_error);\n        {connection_msg, true} ->\n            %% OK - user is authenticated\n            process(Msg);\n        {auth_msg, _} ->\n            %% Auth messages always allowed during handshake\n            handle_auth(Msg)\n    end.\n```\n\n## Common SSH Vulnerabilities\n\n### 1. Pre-Authentication Command Execution (CVE-2025-32433)\n- **Issue**: Connection messages processed before auth\n- **Fix**: Check `authenticated` flag before processing\n\n### 2. Channel ID Confusion\n- **Issue**: Using client-provided channel IDs without validation\n- **Fix**: Validate channel ownership\n\n### 3. Buffer Overflow in Packet Handling\n- **Issue**: Not checking packet size limits\n- **Fix**: Enforce `maximum_packet_size`\n\n### 4. Weak Algorithm Negotiation\n- **Issue**: Accepting deprecated algorithms\n- **Fix**: Configure allowed algorithms list\n\n## Defense in Depth\n\n1. **Check authentication state** at multiple layers\n2. **Validate message sequence** (no out-of-order messages)\n3. **Log suspicious activity** (unexpected messages)\n4. **Rate limit** connection attempts\n5. **Timeout** idle connections\n\n## Secure Disconnect\n\nWhen detecting a protocol violation:\n\n```erlang\n{disconnect,\n {?SSH_DISCONNECT_PROTOCOL_ERROR, \"Descriptive reason\"},\n cleanup_state(Connection)}\n```\n\n- Use appropriate disconnect code\n- Log the violation\n- Clean up connection state\n- Don't leak sensitive information in error messages\n\n## Testing Security Fixes\n\n1. **Positive test**: Normal authentication still works\n2. **Negative test**: Pre-auth messages are rejected\n3. **Boundary test**: Check exact state transition points\n4. **Fuzzing**: Random message sequences\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Timeout) ->\n   369→    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n   370→                 InitialWindowSize,\n   371→                 MaxPacketSize,\n   372→                 Timeout).\n   373→\n   374→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   375→%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n   376→%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n   377→-doc false.\n   378→open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n   379→    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n   380→\n   381→open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n   382→    case       Timeout) of\n   385→        {open, Channel} ->\n   386→\t    {ok, Channel};\n   387→\tError ->\n   388→\t    Error\n   389→    end.\n   390→\n   391→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   392→%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n   393→%% execution of the given command. \n   394→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   395→-doc \"\"\"\n   396→Is to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\n   397→executing the given command. The result is several messages according to the\n   398→following pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\n   399→request is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n   400→\n   401→- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n   402→  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n   403→\n   404→- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n   405→  to be sent.\n   406→\n   407→- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n   408→  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n   409→\n   410→- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n   411→  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n   412→  not always the case.\n   413→\n   414→- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n   415→  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n   416→  shut down.\n   417→\n   418→See the User's Guide section on\n   419→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   420→\"\"\".\n   421→-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n   422→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   423→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   424→      Command :: string(),\n   425→      Timeout :: timeout().\n   426→\n   427→exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n   428→    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n   429→\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n   430→\n   431→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   432→%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n   433→%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n   434→%% end.\n   435→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   436→-doc \"\"\"\n   437→Is to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\n   438→shell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\n   439→server end.\n   440→\n   441→Note: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\n   442→this module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\n   443→would break a large number of existing software.\n   444→\"\"\".\n   445→-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n 464→-spec subsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Subsystem, Timeout) -> result() when\n   465→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   466→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   467→ The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\nevents, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\nchannel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n[below](`t:event/0`)):\n\n`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n\nIf the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior is used to implement the channel process,\nthese messages are handled by\n[handle_ssh_msg/2](`c:ssh_client_channel:handle_ssh_msg/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-moduledoc(#{titles =>\n                 [{type,<<\"General\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Data Transfer\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Closing a Channel\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Environment Variable\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Shell or Command\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Window Change\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Signals\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Exit Status\">>}]}).\n\n-include(\"ssh.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_connect.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_transport.hrl\").\n\n%% API\n-export([session_channel/2, session_channel/4,\n\t exec/4, connection_ref() :: ssh:connection_ref().\n-type channel_id()     :: ssh:channel_id().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe status of a request. Corresponds to the `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` and\n`SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` values in\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.4](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section-5.4).\n\"\"\".\n-type req_status() :: success | failure .\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe result of a call.\n\nIf the request reached the peer, was handled and the response reached the\nrequesting node the `t:req_status/0` is the status reported from the peer.\n\nIf not, the `t:reason/0` indicates what went wrong:\n\n- **`closed`** - indicates that the channel or connection was closed when trying\n  to send the request\n\n- **`timeout`** - indicates that the operation exceeded a time limit\n\"\"\".\n-type reason() :: closed | timeout .\n\n-doc(#{}).\n-type result() :: req_status() | {error, reason()} .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe valid values are `0` (\"normal\") and `1` (\"stderr\"), see\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.2](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#page-8).\n\"\"\".\n-type ssh_data_type_code() :: non_neg_integer(). % Only 0 and 1 are used\n\n\n%%% The SSH Connection Protocol\n\n-export_type([event/0,\n              channel_msg/0,\n              want_reply/0,\n              data_ch_msg/0,\n              eof_ch_msg/0,\n              signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_status_ch_msg/0,\n              closed_ch_msg/0,\n              env_ch_msg/0,\n              pty_ch_msg/0,\n              shell_ch_msg/0,\n              window_change_ch_msg/0,\n              exec_ch_msg/0\n             ]).\n\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type event() :: {ssh_cm, ssh:connection_ref(), channel_msg()}.\n-doc \"\"\"\nAs mentioned in the introduction, the\n[SSH Connection Protocol](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254) events are\nhandled as messages. When writing a channel handling process without using the\nsupport by the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior the process must handle thoose\nmessages.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type channel_msg() ::  data_ch_msg()\n                      | eof_ch_msg()\n                      | closed_ch_msg()\n                      | pty_ch_msg()\n                      | env_ch_msg()\n                      | shell_ch_msg()\n                      | exec_ch_msg()\n                      | signal_ch_msg()\n                      | window_change_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_status_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_signal_ch_msg()\n                        .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nMessages that include a `WantReply` expect the channel handling process to call\n[ssh_connection:reply_request/4](`reply_request/4`) with the boolean value of\n`WantReply` as the second argument.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type want_reply() :: boolean().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nData has arrived on the channel. Some systems do not support signals, in which case they are to ignore\nthis message. There is currently no function to generate this event as the\nsignals referred to are on OS-level and not something generated by an Erlang\nprogram.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Signals\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.9\"}).\n-type signal_ch_msg() :: {signal,\n                          ssh:channel_id(),\n                          SignalName :: string()\n                         } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA remote execution can terminate violently because of a signal.             } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nEnvironment variables can be passed to the shell/command to be started later.\nThis event is sent as a result of calling <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n-type pty_ch_msg() :: {pty,\n                       ssh:channel_id(),\n                       want_reply(),\n                      PixelHeight :: non_neg_integer(),\n                        TerminalModes :: [term_mode()]\n                       }\n                      } .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nA pseudo-terminal has been requested for the session. `Terminal` is the value of\nthe TERM environment variable value, that is, `vt100`. Zero dimension parameters\nmust be ignored. The character/row dimensions override the pixel dimensions\n(when non-zero). Pixel dimensions refer to the drawable area of the window.\n`Opcode` in the `TerminalModes` list is the mnemonic name, represented as a\nlowercase Erlang atom, defined in\n[RFC 4254](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section/8), Section 8. It can\nalso be an `Opcode` if the mnemonic name is not listed in the RFC. Example:\n`OP code: 53, mnemonic name ECHO erlang atom: echo`. A session is a\n%% remote execution of a program. The program may be a shell, an\n%% application, a system command, or some built-in subsystem.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n-doc(#{equiv => session_channel/4}).\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) ->\n    session_channel(ConnectionRef, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nOpens a channel for an SSH session. The channel id returned from this function\nis the id used as input to the other functions in this module.\n\"\"\".\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      InitialWindowSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      MaxPacketSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n                 InitialWindowSize,\n                 MaxPacketSize,\n                 Timeout).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc false.\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    case ssh_connection_handler:open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData,\n                                             InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize,\n                                             Timeout) of\n        {open, Channel} ->\n\t    {ok, Channel};\n\tError ->\n\t    Error\n    end.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n%% execution of the given command. \n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\nexecuting the given command. The result is several messages according to the\nfollowing pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\nrequest is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n\n- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n  to be sent.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n  not always the case.\n\n- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n  shut down.\n\nSee the User's Guide section on\n[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n\"\"\".\n-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Command :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nexec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n%% end.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\nshell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\nserver end.\n\nNote: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\nthis module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\nwould break a large number of existing software.\n\"\"\".\n-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Result :: ok | success | failure | {error, timeout} .\n\nshell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) ->\n PixHeight});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"signal\",\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data}, \n           Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(SigName, _SigLen)>> = Data,\n    reply_msg(ChannelId, Connection0, {signal, ChannelId,\n                                       binary_to_list(SigName)});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"subsystem\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(SsName,_SsLen)>> = Data,\n    #channel{remote_id=RemoteId} = Channel = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId), \n    Reply =\n        case start_subsystem(SsName, Connection, Channel,\n                             {subsystem, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(SsName)}) of\n            {ok, Pid} ->\n                erlang:monitor(process, Pid),\n                ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel#channel{user=Pid}),\n                channel_success_msg(RemoteId);\n            {error,_Error} ->\n                channel_failure_msg(RemoteId)\n        end,\n    {[{connection_reply,Reply}], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"subsystem\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore subsystem requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"pty-req\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(BTermName,_TermLen),\n      ?UINT32(Width),?UINT32(Height),\n      ?UINT32(PixWidth), ?UINT32(PixHeight),\n      Modes/binary>> = Data,\n    TermName = binary_to_list(BTermName),\n    PtyOpts0 = decode_pty_opts(Modes),\n    PtyOpts = case proplists:get_value(onlcr, PtyOpts0, undefined) of\n                  undefined ->\n                      %% If - peer client asked for pty\n                      %%    - did not tell if LF->CRLF expansion is wanted\n                      %% then\n                      %%    - do LF->CRLF expansion\n                      [{onlcr,1} | PtyOpts0];\n                  _ ->\n                      PtyOpts0\n              end,\n    PtyRequest = {TermName, Width, Height,\n\t\t  PixWidth, PixHeight, PtyOpts},\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {pty, ChannelId, WantReply, PtyRequest});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"pty-req\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore pty requests. See RFC 4254 6.2.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"shell\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {shell, ChannelId, WantReply});\n \nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"shell\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore shell requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n\t\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"exec\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore exec requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n  \t\t\t\t    request_type = \"env\",\n  \t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n  \t\t\t\t    data = Data}, \n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Var,_VarLen), ?DEC_BIN(Value,_ValLen)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n \t\t   {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"env\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore env requests. \n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    %% Not a valid request_type. All valid types are handling the\n    %% parameter checking in their own clauses above.\n    %% \n    %% The special ReqType faulty_msg signals that something went\n    %% wrong found during decoding.\n    %%\n    %% RFC4254 5.4 says:\n    %% \"If 'want reply' is FALSE, no response will be sent to the request.\n    %%  Otherwise, the recipient responds with either\n    %%  SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, or request-specific\n    %%  continuation messages.  If the request is not recognized or is not\n    %%  supported for the channel, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE is returned.\"\n    %%\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} when WantReply==true -> \n            FailMsg = channel_failure_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n        _ -> %% Channel has been closed or no reply is wanted\n            {[], Connection}\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = <<\"tcpip-forward\">>,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = <<?DEC_BIN(ListenAddrStr,_Len),?UINT32(ListenPort)>>},\n           #connection{options = Opts} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(tcpip_tunnel_out, Opts) of\n        false ->\n            %% This daemon instance has not enabled tcpip_forwarding\n            {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n        true ->\n            ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n            FwdSup = ssh_connection_sup:tcpip_fwd_supervisor(ConnectionSup),\n            ConnPid = self(),\n            case ssh_tcpip_forward_acceptor:supervised_start(FwdSup,\n                                                             {ListenAddrStr, ListenPort},\n                                                             undefined,\n                                                             \"forwarded-tcpip\", ssh_tcpip_forward_srv,\n                                                             ConnPid) of\n                {ok,ListenPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {ok,LPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<?UINT32(LPort)>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {error,_} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ ->\n                    {[], Connection}\n            end\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = _Type,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = _Data}, Connection, _Role, _SSH) ->\n    if WantReply == true ->\n\t    FailMsg = request_failure_msg(),\n\t    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n       true ->\n\t    {[], Connection}  \n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From,_} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From, Fun} | Rest]} = Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    Connection = Fun({success,Data}, Connection0),\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_disconnect{code = Code,\n\t\t\t       description = Description},\n\t   Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {disconnect, {Code, Description}, handle_stop(Connection)}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Returns pending responses to be delivered to the peer when a\n%%% Channel/Connection closes\n%%%\n-doc false.\nhandle_stop(#connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection0) ->\n    {Connection, Replies} = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(\n          fun(Channel, {Connection1, Acc}) ->\n                  {Reply, Connection2} =\n                      reply_msg(Channel, Connection1,\n                                {closed, Channel#channel.local_id}),\n                  {Connection2, Reply ++ Acc}\n          end, {Connection0, []}, Cache),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_delete(Cache),\n    {Replies, Connection}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% channel_*_msg(...)\n%%% Returns a #ssh_msg_....{} for channel operations.\n%%%\n-doc false.\nchannel_adjust_window_msg(ChannelId, Bytes) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_window_adjust{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t   bytes_to_add = Bytes}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_close_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_close {recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, 0, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  data = Data};\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_extended_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    data_type_code = Type,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_eof_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_eof{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_failure_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_failure{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_msg(Type, ChannelId, WindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = Type,\n\t\t\t  sender_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t  maximum_packet_size = MaxPacketSize,\n\t\t\t  data = Data\n\t\t\t }.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_confirmation_msg(RemoteId, LID, WindowSize, PacketSize) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open_confirmation{recipient_channel = RemoteId,\n\t\t\t\t       sender_channel = LID,\n\t\t\t\t       initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t       maximum_packet_size = PacketSize}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, Reason, Description, Lang) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open_failure{recipient_channel = RemoteId,\n\t\t\t\t  reason = Reason,\n\t\t\t\t  description = Description,\n\t\t\t\t  lang = Lang}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_status_msg({success, #ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%%\n-doc false.\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_tuple(Addr) ->\n    case catch inet_parse:ntoa(Addr) of\n\t{'EXIT',_} -> false;\n\tA -> A\n    end;\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_list(Addr) ->\n    case inet_parse:address(Addr) of\n\t{ok, _} -> Addr;\n\tError ->\n\t    case inet:getaddr(Addr, inet) of\n\t\t{ok, A} ->\n\t\t    inet_parse:ntoa(A);\n\t\tError -> false\n\t    end\n    end.\n\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n%%%\n%%% Internal functions\n%%%\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Create the channel data when an ssh_msg_open_channel message\n%%% of \"session\" typ is handled\n%%%\nsetup_session(#connection{channel_cache = Cache,\n                          channel_id_seed = NewChannelID,\n                          suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n                          suggest_packet_size = PktSz\n\t\t\t } = C,\n\t      RemoteId, Type, WindowSize, PacketSize) when is_integer(WinSz),\n                                                           is_integer(PktSz) ->\n    NextChannelID = NewChannelID + 1,\n    Channel =\n        #channel{type = Type,\n                 sys = \"ssh\",\n                 local_id = NewChannelID,\n                 recv_window_size = WinSz,\n                 recv_packet_size = PktSz,\n                 send_window_size = WindowSize,\n                 send_packet_size = NextChannelID}}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Start a cli or subsystem\n%%%\nstart_cli(#connection{options = Options, \n\t\t      cli_spec = CliSpec,\n\t\t      exec = Exec,\n\t\t      connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup}, ChannelId) ->\n    case CliSpec of\n        no_cli ->\n            {error, cli_disabled};\n        {CbModule, Args} ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), CbModule, ChannelId, Args, Exec, Options)\n    end.\n\n\nstart_subsystem(BinName, #connection{options = Options,\n                                     connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup},\n\t       #channel{local_id = ChannelId}, _ReplyMsg) ->\n    Name = binary_to_list(BinName),\n    case check_subsystem(Name, Options) of\n\t{Callback, Opts} when is_atom(Callback), Callback =/= none ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), Callback, ChannelId, Opts, undefined, Options);\n        {none, _} ->\n            {error, bad_subsystem};\n\t{_, _} ->\n\t    {error, legacy_option_not_supported}\n    end.\n\n\n%%% Helpers for starting cli/subsystems\ncheck_subsystem(\"sftp\"= SsName, Options) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options) of\n\tno_subsys -> \t% FIXME: Can 'no_subsys' ever be matched?\n\t    {SsName, {Cb, Opts}} = ssh_sftpd:subsystem_spec([]),\n\t    {Cb, Opts};\n\tSubSystems ->\n\t    proplists:get_value(SsName, SubSystems, {none, []})\n    end;\n\ncheck_subsystem(SsName, Options) ->\n    Subsystems = ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options),\n    case proplists:get_value(SsName, Subsystems, {none, []}) of\n\tFun when is_function(Fun) ->\n\t    {Fun, []};\n\t{_, _} = Value ->\n\t    Value\n    end.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Send-window handling\n%%%\n\nupdate_send_window(Channel, _, undefined,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel, Cache);\n\nupdate_send_window(#channel{send_buf = SendBuffer} = Channel, DataType, Data,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel#channel{send_buf = queue:in({DataType, Data}, SendBuffer)},\n\t\t\t  Cache).\n\ndo_update_send_window(Channel0, Cache) ->\n    {SendMsgs, Channel} = get_window(Channel0, []),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel), \n    {SendMsgs, Channel}.\n\nget_window(#channel{send_window_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_packet_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_buf = Buffer, \n\t\t    send_packet_size = PacketSize,\n\t\t    send_window_size = WindowSize0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc0) ->\n    case queue:out(Buffer) of\n\t{{value, {_, Data} = Msg}, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    case handle_send_window(Msg, byte_size(Data), PacketSize, WindowSize0, Acc0) of\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, {_, <<>>}} ->\n\t\t    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t send_buf = NewBuffer}};\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, Rest} ->\n\t\t    get_window(Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t       send_buf = queue:in_r(Rest, NewBuffer)}, Acc)\n\t    end;\n\t{empty, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    {[], Channel#channel{send_buf = NewBuffer}}\n    end.\n\nhandle_send_window(Msg = {Type, Data}, Size, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when Size =< WindowSize ->\n    case Size =< PacketSize of\n\ttrue ->\n\t    {WindowSize - Size, [Msg | Acc], {Type, <<>>}};\n\tfalse ->\n\t    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n\t    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}\n    end;\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when WindowSize =< PacketSize ->\n    <<Msg1:WindowSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - WindowSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}};\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) ->\n    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Flow control\n%%% \n\nflow_control(Channel, Cache) ->\n [].\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Pseudo terminal stuff\n%%% \n\npty_req(ConnectionRef, Channel, Term, Width, Height,\n\t PixWidth, PixHeight, <<?STRING(Bin)>>.\n\nencode_pty_opts2([]) -> \n    [?TTY_OP_END];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vintr,Value} | Opts]) ->\n    [?VINTR, ?uint32(Value) | encode_pty_opts2(Opts)];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vquit,Value} The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\nevents, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\nchannel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n[below](`t:event/0`)):\n\n`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n\nIf the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior is used to [{type,<<\"General\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Data Transfer\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Closing a Channel\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>},\n {type,<<\"Exit Status\">>}]}).\n\n-include(\"ssh.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_connect.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_transport.hrl\").\n\n%% API\n-export([session_channel/2, session_channel/4,\n\t exec/4, Corresponds to the `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` and\n`SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` values in\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.4](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section-5.4).\n\"\"\".\n-type req_status() :: success | failure .\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe result of a call.\n\nIf the request reached the peer, was handled and the response reached the\nrequesting node the `t:req_status/0` is the status reported from the peer.\n\nIf not, the `t:reason/0` indicates what went wrong:\n\n- **`closed`** - indicates that the channel or connection was closed when trying\n  to send the request\n\n- **`timeout`** - indicates that the operation exceeded a time limit\n\"\"\".\n-type reason() :: closed | timeout .\n\n-doc(#{}).\n-type result() :: req_status() | {error, reason()} .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe valid values are `0` (\"normal\") and `1` (\"stderr\"), see\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.2](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#page-8).\n\"\"\".\n-type ssh_data_type_code() :: non_neg_integer(). % Only 0 and 1 are used\n\n\n%%% The SSH Connection Protocol\n\n-export_type([event/0,\n              channel_msg/0,\n              want_reply/0,\n              data_ch_msg/0,\n              eof_ch_msg/0,\n              signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_status_ch_msg/0,\n              closed_ch_msg/0,\n              env_ch_msg/0,\n              pty_ch_msg/0,\n              shell_ch_msg/0,\n              window_change_ch_msg/0,\n              exec_ch_msg/0\n             ]).\n\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type event() :: {ssh_cm, ssh:connection_ref(), channel_msg()}.\n-doc \"\"\"\nAs mentioned in the introduction, the\n[SSH Connection Protocol](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254) events are\nhandled as messages. When writing a channel handling process without using the\nsupport by the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior the process must handle thoose\nmessages.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type channel_msg() ::  data_ch_msg()\n                      | eof_ch_msg()\n                      | closed_ch_msg()\n                      | pty_ch_msg()\n                      | env_ch_msg()\n                      | shell_ch_msg()\n                      | exec_ch_msg()\n                      | signal_ch_msg()\n                      | window_change_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_status_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_signal_ch_msg()\n                        .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nMessages that include a `WantReply` expect the channel handling process to call\n[ssh_connection:reply_request/4](`reply_request/4`) with the boolean value of\n`WantReply` as the second argument.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type want_reply() :: boolean().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nData has arrived on the channel. Some systems do not support signals, in which case they are to ignore\nthis message. There is currently no function to generate this event as the\nsignals referred to are on OS-level and not something generated by an Erlang\nprogram.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Signals\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.9\"}).\n-type signal_ch_msg() :: {signal,\n                          ssh:channel_id(),\n                          SignalName :: string()\n                         } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA remote execution can terminate violently because of a signal. <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n-type pty_ch_msg() :: {pty,\n                       ssh:channel_id(),\n                       want_reply(),\n                      PixelHeight :: non_neg_integer(),\n                        TerminalModes :: [term_mode()]\n                       }\n                      } .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nA pseudo-terminal has been requested for the session. `Terminal` is the value of\nthe TERM environment variable value, that is, `vt100`. Zero dimension parameters\nmust be ignored. The character/row dimensions override the pixel dimensions\n(when non-zero). Pixel dimensions refer to the drawable area of the window.\n`Opcode` in the `TerminalModes` list is the mnemonic name, represented as a\nlowercase Erlang atom, defined in\n[RFC 4254](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section/8), Section 8. A session is a\n%% remote execution of a program. The program may be a shell, an\n%% application, a system command, or some built-in subsystem.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n-doc(#{equiv => session_channel/4}).\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) ->\n    session_channel(ConnectionRef, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nOpens a channel for an SSH session. The channel id returned from this function\nis the id used as input to the other functions in this module.\n\"\"\".\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      InitialWindowSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      MaxPacketSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n                 InitialWindowSize,\n                 MaxPacketSize,\n                 Timeout).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc false.\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    case ssh_connection_handler:open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData,\n                                             InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize,\n                                             Timeout) of\n        {open, Channel} ->\n\t    {ok, Channel};\n\tError ->\n\t    Error\n    end.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n%% execution of the given command. \n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\nexecuting the given command. The result is several messages according to the\nfollowing pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\nrequest is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n\n- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n  to be sent.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n  not always the case.\n\n- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n  shut down.\n\nSee the User's Guide section on\n[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n\"\"\".\n-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Command :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nexec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n%% end.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\nshell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\nserver end.\n\nNote: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\nthis module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\nwould break a large number of existing software.\n\"\"\".\n-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Result :: ok | success | failure | {error, timeout} .\n\nshell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId,\n \t\t\t\t   \"shell\", false, <<>>, 0).\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%%\n%% Description: Executes a predefined subsystem.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process for requesting to execute a\npredefined subsystem on the server.\n\nThe function [`subsystem/4`](`subsystem/4`) and subsequent calls of\n[send/3,4,5](`send/3`) must be executed in the same process.\n\"\"\".\n-spec subsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Subsystem, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Subsystem  :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nsubsystem(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, SubSystem, TimeOut) ->\n     ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(),\n\t\t\t\t    ChannelId, \"subsystem\", \n\t\t\t\t    true, [?string(SubSystem)], TimeOut).\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Sends channel data.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc(#{equiv => send/5}).\n-spec send(connection_ref(), channel_id(), iodata()) ->\n\t\t  ok | {error, reason()}.\n\nsend(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Data) ->\n    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, infinity).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nsend(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data)\n\nDepending on input arguments equivalent to one of `send/5` calls specified below.\n\nEquivalent to [send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, TimeOut)](`send/5`) if\ncalled with TimeOut being integer.\n\nEquivalent to [send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, infinity)](`send/5`) if\ncalled with TimeOut being infinity atom.\n\nEquivalent to [send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data, infinity)](`send/5`) if\ncalled with last argument which is not integer or infinity atom.\n\"\"\".\n\n-spec send(connection_ref(), channel_id(), iodata(), timeout()) -> ok | {error, reason()};\n          (connection_ref(), channel_id(), ssh_data_type_code(), iodata()) -> ok | {error, reason()}.\n\nsend(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Data, TimeOut) when is_integer(TimeOut) ->\n    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, TimeOut);\n\nsend(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Data, infinity) ->\n    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, 0, Data, infinity);\n\nsend(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n    send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Type, Data, infinity).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by client- and server-channel processes to send data to each\nother.\n\nThe function `subsystem/4` and subsequent calls of ChannelId, Type, Data, TimeOut) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:send(ConnectionRef, ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\tType, Data, TimeOut).\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"Sends EOF on channel `ChannelId`.\".\n-spec send_eof(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> ok  | {error, closed} when\n _LangLen)>> = Data,\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} = Channel ->\n            {Reply, Connection} =  reply_msg(Channel,   binary_to_list(Err),\n                                              binary_to_list(Lang)}),\n            ChannelCloseMsg = channel_close_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, binary_to_list(SigName)});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"subsystem\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"pty-req\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(BTermName,_TermLen),\n      ?UINT32(Width),?UINT32(Height),\n      ?UINT32(PixWidth), ?UINT32(PixHeight),\n      Modes/binary>> = Data,\n    TermName = binary_to_list(BTermName),\n    PtyOpts0 = decode_pty_opts(Modes),\n    PtyOpts = case proplists:get_value(onlcr, PtyOpts0, undefined) of\n                  undefined ->\n                      %% If - peer client asked for pty\n                      %%    - did not tell if LF->CRLF expansion is wanted\n                      %% then\n                      %%    - do LF->CRLF expansion\n                      [{onlcr,1} | PtyOpts0];\n                  _ ->\n                      PtyOpts0\n              end,\n    PtyRequest = {TermName, Width, Height,\n\t\t  PixWidth, PixHeight, PtyOpts},\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {pty, ChannelId, WantReply, PtyRequest});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"pty-req\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore pty requests. See RFC 4254 6.2.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"shell\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {shell, ChannelId, WantReply});\n \nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"shell\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore shell requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n\t\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"exec\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore exec requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n  \t\t\t\t    request_type = \"env\",\n  \t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n  \t\t\t\t    data = Data}, \n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Var,_VarLen), ?DEC_BIN(Value,_ValLen)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n \t\t   {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"env\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore env requests. \n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    %% Not a valid request_type. All valid types are handling the\n    %% parameter checking in their own clauses above.\n    %% \n    %% The special ReqType faulty_msg signals that something went\n    %% wrong found during decoding.\n    %%\n    %% RFC4254 5.4 says:\n    %% \"If 'want reply' is FALSE, no response will be sent to the request.\n    %%  Otherwise, the recipient responds with either\n    %%  SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, or request-specific\n    %%  continuation messages.  If the request is not recognized or is not\n    %%  supported for the channel, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE is returned.\"\n    %%\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} when WantReply==true -> \n            FailMsg = channel_failure_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n        _ -> %% Channel has been closed or no reply is wanted\n            {[], Connection}\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = <<\"tcpip-forward\">>,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = <<?DEC_BIN(ListenAddrStr,_Len),?UINT32(ListenPort)>>},\n           #connection{options = Opts} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(tcpip_tunnel_out, Opts) of\n        false ->\n            %% This daemon instance has not enabled tcpip_forwarding\n            {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n        true ->\n            ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n            FwdSup = ssh_connection_sup:tcpip_fwd_supervisor(ConnectionSup),\n            ConnPid = self(),\n            case ssh_tcpip_forward_acceptor:supervised_start(FwdSup,\n                                                             {ListenAddrStr, ListenPort},\n                                                             undefined,\n                                                             \"forwarded-tcpip\", ssh_tcpip_forward_srv,\n                                                             ConnPid) of\n                {ok,ListenPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {ok,LPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<?UINT32(LPort)>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {error,_} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ ->\n                    {[], Connection}\n            end\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = _Type,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = _Data}, Connection, _Role, _SSH) ->\n    if WantReply == true ->\n\t    FailMsg = request_failure_msg(),\n\t    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n       true ->\n\t    {[], Connection}  \n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From,_} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From, Fun} | Rest]} = Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    Connection = Fun({success,Data}, Connection0),\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_disconnect{code = Code,\n\t\t\t       description = Description},\n\t   Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {disconnect, {Code, Description}, handle_stop(Connection)}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Returns pending responses to be delivered to the peer when a\n%%% Channel/Connection closes\n%%%\n-doc false.\nhandle_stop(#connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection0) ->\n    {Connection, Replies} = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(\n          fun(Channel, {Connection1, Acc}) ->\n                  {Reply, Connection2} =\n                      reply_msg(Channel, Connection1,\n                                {closed, Channel#channel.local_id}),\n                  {Connection2, Reply ++ Acc}\n          end, {Connection0, []}, Cache),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_delete(Cache),\n    {Replies, Connection}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% channel_*_msg(...)\n%%% Returns a #ssh_msg_....{} for channel operations.\n%%%\n-doc false.\nchannel_adjust_window_msg(ChannelId, Bytes) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_window_adjust{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t   bytes_to_add = Bytes}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_close_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_close {recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, 0, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  data = Data};\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_extended_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_failure{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_msg(Type, ChannelId, WindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = false.\nchannel_open_confirmation_msg(RemoteId, LID, WindowSize, PacketSize) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open_confirmation{recipient_channel = RemoteId,\n\t\t\t\t       sender_channel = LID,\n\t\t\t\t #ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%%\n-doc false.\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_tuple(Addr) ->\n    case catch inet_parse:ntoa(Addr) of\n\t{'EXIT',_} -> false;\n\tA -> A\n    end;\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_list(Addr) ->\n    case inet_parse:address(Addr) of\n\t{ok, _} -> Addr;\n\tError ->\n\t    case inet:getaddr(Addr, inet) of\n\t\t{ok, A} ->\n\t\t    inet_parse:ntoa(A);\n\t\tError -> false\n\t    end\n    end.\n\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n%%%\n%%% Internal functions\n%%%\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Create the channel data when an ssh_msg_open_channel message\n%%% of \"session\" typ is handled\n%%%\nsetup_session(#connection{channel_cache = Cache,\n                          channel_id_seed = NewChannelID,\n                          suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n                          suggest_packet_size = PktSz\n\t\t\t } = C,\n\t      RemoteId, Type, WindowSize, PacketSize) when is_integer(WinSz),\n                                                           is_integer(PktSz) ->\n    NextChannelID = NewChannelID + 1,\n    Channel =\n        #channel{type = Type,\n                 sys = \"ssh\",\n                 local_id
